# Audit of v1–v3 for deployment (V4-1)

The owner intends to run this protocol as the login system for one website.
Everything in v1–v3 was built and verified as a **reference implementation**
whose own README says *"Not production-ready"*. This audit asks a different
question of the same code: **what stands between it and that deployment?**

Method: three passes — mechanical (re-runnable scripts under `tools/audit/`,
each with a demonstrated failure mode), a fresh full read, and an adversarial
pass per entry point — plus a disposition of the 31 debt items recorded across
v1–v3. Nothing is fixed here. Every finding gets a severity *for this
deployment*, evidence as `file:line`, and an owning step.

Scope of the surface: **3,603 lines** of library (`src/crypto` 748,
`src/protocol` 2,756, `src/util` 99), **2,370** of apps, 7,599 of tests, 3,168
of fuzz harness, 433 of tools. 29 commits since `v1.0.0`; v1's protocol code
was deleted from `main` in V2-4/V2-5, so v1 is audited through its frozen spec
and its decisions, not its code.

## Findings register

Severity is for *this* deployment (one website, operators then public users),
not in the abstract.

| # | Finding | Era | Sev | Evidence | Disposition | Step |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **F0** | **Release + gcc does not build on Linux.** `-Werror=unused-result` on two `symlink()` calls. gcc does **not** honour a `(void)` cast on `warn_unused_result`; clang does. `_FORTIFY_SOURCE=2` is only defined when not sanitizing and only bites at `-O2`, so Debug/ASan/UBSan never see it. **CI builds Linux only in Debug/ASan/UBSan; the sole Release build is `bench.yml`, with clang.** The natural deployment build has never been attempted. | v2 | **High** | `tests/test_net.c:1849,1959`; `CMakeLists.txt:75-78`; `.github/workflows/ci.yml:35-43`; `bench.yml:53`; measured: `Release+gcc FAILS`, `Release+clang BUILDS`, gcc warns on `(void)symlink` / clang does not | **FIXED** in two halves, and the row left saying neither until V4-20: both `symlink()` calls check their return (`176d786`, V4-5; now `tests/test_net.c:1849,1961`), and CI's `release` job (V4-11, `.github/workflows/ci.yml:187`) builds Release with **gcc and clang**, runs the whole suite, installs, and gates the install tree with `check_hardening.sh --require` -- so the deployment build is attempted on every push | V4-5 / V4-11 |
| F1 | **Successful handshakes cap at capacity ÷ TTL.** A completed handshake leaves a CONSUMED tombstone reclaimed only at expiry; `cancel` refuses an unexpired tombstone. 16 per 30 s as the demo is configured; 256 per 30 s at the compile-time maximum. Replay protection working as designed, but it is a hard throughput ceiling. `handshake_pending_active_count` counts only ACTIVE entries, so it reports 0 while the store is full. | v2 | **High** (B) | `src/protocol/handshake.c` insert/sweep ~110-160, consume/cancel ~265-305; `handshake.h:73` | **FIXED in V4-12.** `handshake_pending_store_init_ext` takes a caller-provided backing array, so the daemon sizes the ledger from its own `max_slots` instead of being clamped to the inline 256. Capacity is capped at a **measured** 2,048, not a round number: `find_slot` is a constant-time full scan, and while writing that constant's comment the walks were counted and V4-2's S2 model found to undercount — a successful responder handshake performs **five** full walks (sweep, `find_slot` and the placement loop inside `insert()`, then one `find_slot` each in `get_digest()` and `consume_success()`), not four, putting 2,048 at ~99.5 µs against the 100 µs budget rather than 79.6. The sustained rate rises 8×, from 25.6/s to 205/s at the default 10 s timeout. The tombstone behaviour itself is unchanged and still correct: it is the replay record | V4-8 / V4-12 |
| F2 | **Enrolled identities enumerate.** An unknown identity is closed with no ServerHello; a pinned one gets ~4.5 KB. One connection per guess, and the unknown path is also much cheaper (no keygen, encaps or signature). | v2 | **High** | `src/protocol/handshake.c:863-867`; `apps/demo_app.c:253-258` | **CLOSED in V4-10b.** Verified for this sweep, not assumed: every lookup miss -- unknown, revoked, superseded, disabled -- lands in one branch and gets the decoy pin (`authd_conn.c:191`), and the per-address token bucket (`ratelimit.c`) now pays for the signature each probe costs, which is the half §7.3 always said was the limiter's | V4-8 |
| F3 | `keystore_lookup` early-exits on a hit; a miss always scans 32 entries. Deliberately *not* how `find_slot` is written (constant-time, no early exit, comment at `handshake.c:74`). | v1 | Low | `src/protocol/keystore.c:19-26,82-88` vs `handshake.c:74-90` | **CLOSED by design, re-checked in V4-11.** The daemon never enumerates the in-memory keystore: it resolves the identity in the store first and adds exactly one entry to a per-slot scratch table, so `keystore_lookup`'s early exit runs over a single element and has nothing to distinguish | V4-7 |
| F4 | **No PIE/RELRO/BIND_NOW/noexecstack in the project's own link options.** macOS supplies PIE, NX and canaries by default; RELRO and BIND_NOW are Linux concepts and are the deployment platform's. | v1 | **Med** | `CMakeLists.txt:62-78`; `src/CMakeLists.txt:24`, `apps/CMakeLists.txt:13,19,25` (sanitizer flags only); measured by `tools/audit/check_hardening.sh` | **FIXED in V4-11.** Measured rather than assumed first: of the five properties only BIND_NOW was actually missing, the rest being toolchain defaults. `-Wl,-z,relro,-z,now,-z,noexecstack` and PIE are now probed with `check_linker_flag`/`check_pie_supported` and applied per target, so a toolchain that rejects them (macOS rejects `-z` outright) simply does not get them, and `check_hardening.sh --require` gates the **install tree** on Linux | V4-11 |
| F5 | **Coverage had never been measured.** Now measured (below). | v1–v3 | Med | `tools/audit/coverage.sh` | gaps → tests. **Still open, stated in V4-20:** measured once, here, before the daemon existed, and never since -- nothing under `apps/authd/` has a coverage figure. Coverage was deliberately not made a gate (the campaigns are), and the gaps this row meant were taken up as the claim map's "not established" entries (V4-15a–c), each closed by a check shown able to fail rather than by a percentage. **MEASURED in V4-40** (`docs/v4/coverage.md`; macOS, Apple clang 21, all 56 tests, the new `-DMLDSA_COVERAGE=ON`): `apps/authd/` 81.30% of lines, 87.51% of regions, 69.05% of branches, 97.45% of functions; `src/` 83.67%, 92.02%, 73.37%, 100%. Measuring it first found that forked test children were never counted (`_exit()` skips the profile write; `cli_prompt.c` read 4.5% of lines, 67.2% once counted). It stays open: the figure names five security checks no test reaches (F122), and the wasm build is not measured. **V4-50:** four of F122's five are covered (V4-42, V4-43, V4-45, V4-46), with re-measured figures in `docs/v4/coverage.md`, and V4-48 and V4-49 took two more files the figures pointed at (the passphrase file, the listener's paths). What keeps F5 open is F122 (3), `REVOKE-TOKENS handle=`, which waits on the owner's decision on F123, and the unmeasured wasm build. **V4-60:** F122 (3) is covered, with F123's fix; only the unmeasured wasm build keeps F5 open | V4-5, V4-40, V4-50, V4-60 |
| F6 | `mlock` is never called by this code; it relies on libsodium's `sodium_malloc`, which **ignores `mlock` failure silently**. Guard pages are attested only indirectly (mutation K4b SegFault). ~10 such blocks per handshake. | v1 | Med | `src/util/secure_mem.{h,c}`; `handshake.c:418,668,770,785,797,933,1089,1106`; `kex.c:15`; `mlkem_wrap.c:43` | **FIXED in V4-11.** The unit sets `LimitMEMLOCK`, and -- because a unit only protects operators who use the unit -- the daemon reads `RLIMIT_MEMLOCK` itself at startup and logs it, at WARN when it is under the derived need. It warns rather than refuses: Linux's usual 8 MiB default is already short at the default 256 slots, so refusing would convert a widespread misconfiguration into an outage. Campaign v51 (Z1-Z5) | V4-2 / V4-11 |
| F7 | No `install()` target anywhere; static libs; default build type **Debug**; default `MLDSA_OQS_OPT_TARGET=auto` = `-march=native`, documented as crashing on an older CPU of the same family. | v1/v2 | **Med** | `src/CMakeLists.txt:1`; `apps/CMakeLists.txt:3`; `CMakeLists.txt:4-10,90-92` | **FIXED in V4-11.** `install(TARGETS ...)` for the three shipped binaries plus the unit and example config; the README and runbook give the Release recipe with a **named** CPU target (`x86-64-v3`) rather than the non-portable `auto` default | V4-11 |
| F8 | ~10 `sodium_malloc`/`free` pairs per handshake (mmap + guard pages each). The *session* steady state is allocation-free and gated; the handshake is not. | v2 | Low | call sites as F6; `session.h:66-73` | **CLOSED in V4-12 with a number.** `bench/bench_authd.c` counts them with the same counting allocator `test_session_alloc` uses — reused rather than copied, so it cannot drift from the real allocator — and proves the counter is live (`allocs > 0`) before trusting the balance, since `0 == 0` is also what an unwired counter reports. A handshake performs **12 secure allocations and 12 frees**, balanced; the register's estimate was "~10". The bench fails outright if they ever differ, so a leak becomes a gate rather than a note | V4-12 |
| F9 | Security Req 4.7 says re-registration with a different key is "rejected **and logged**". The library rejects (`KEYSTORE_ERR_KEY_MISMATCH`) and explicitly delegates the logging half to the caller — which no caller implements. | v1 | **Med** | `src/protocol/keystore.h:25-29,60-65`; `keystore.c:46-54`; `docs/decisions.md:180-187` | **CLOSED in V4-9a**, verified for this sweep at `store/store.c:828`: a known handle presenting a different key writes an `enroll-key-mismatch` row inside the same transaction that refuses it, which is Req 7's "rejected **and audited**". *Superseded (F98, V4-15a):* since F47 that row is rolled back on both daemon paths; Req 7's log is the journal (erratum 45) | V4-9 |
| F10 | Loopback IPv4 only; `accept(fd, NULL, NULL)` so the peer address is never obtained; no IPv6 path exists. | v1 | **Med** | `apps/net_io.c:73-78,118-142,154` | **CLOSED in V4-10b.** The client address arrives by PROXY protocol v2 on the proxy-facing Unix socket, fails closed when absent, and reaches the limiter, the log and the store (`authd_conn.c:86`) | V4-8 / V4-10 |
| F11 | No in-session rekey: `session_rekey_due()` means "run a new handshake", and the demo just closes. | v1 | Low | `session.h:253-255`; `demo_app.c:356-364` | accept; sessions here are seconds | — |
| F12 | **The build needs the network at configure time** (liboqs clone + libsodium tarball). `-DFETCHCONTENT_SOURCE_DIR_LIBOQS` covers liboqs only; no documented offline path for libsodium. | v2 | Med | `cmake/Dependencies.cmake:61-71,139-143`; `README.md:55-57` | **FIXED in V4-11.** `MLDSA_DEPS_CACHE` + `deploy/fetch-deps.sh`, with every pin still enforced -- an offline build is checked three times and an online one twice. Proven by building with `--network none` in a container, with controls for a corrupted archive, a cache off the pinned commit, and an unreadable one (F72) | V4-11 |
| F13 | The tree must not live under a path containing spaces (libtool). The current working copy **violates this** (`…/PQ Authentication protocol/…`) and it will also bite the wasm spike. | v2 | Low | `cmake/Dependencies.cmake:108-125` | operational constraint; build elsewhere | V4-2 / V4-11 **Carried into the runbook in V4-11** rather than left in the register: step 1 states the constraint where someone is about to choose a build directory, which is the only moment it can be acted on. |
| F14 | Identities are logged (escaped). For operators that is an audit feature; for **public end users a handle in a log is PII**. | v2 | Med | `apps/demo_app.c:45-57,262,437`; `demo_app.h:30-33` | **Recorded, and its proposed mechanism does not exist.** V4-10c's F46 established that `log_identities` and `log_client_ip` are named in spec §15 and implemented nowhere, so the policy this row assumed was already available has never been written. Re-checked in V4-11 and left open deliberately: the daemon's own logging is minimal and the operator population is small, and the decision of what an end-user deployment may record belongs with the browser client (V4-13), where the users whose handles these are actually appear. **RESOLVED in V4-13d** (spec v1.7 errata 36, 37): `log_identities = full \| hashed \| off` and `log_client_ip = full \| prefix \| off`, both defaulting to `full` so no existing deployment changes; `hashed` writes `idh=`, a keyed BLAKE2b pseudonym under `key_log = HKDF(KEK, store_id, "mldsa-authd/v1/log-pseudonym")`, which never leaves the store; `authd_admin pseudonym` computes one offline for an operator holding the passphrase. The store's audit chain keeps the real identifiers under every setting. Proven by `test_authd_log`, `test_authd_localapi` (a whole PROXY v2 login), `authd_e2e` (the shipped daemon and `pseudonym` agree) and `caddy_proxy.sh` (through a real Caddy); campaign v56 MB1–MB10 | V4-13d |
| F15 | v1 spec §5.1's x86_64 target was never measured on x86_64; v1's code is gone, so it cannot be. | v1 | Info | `docs/decisions.md:650-657`; `bench/results.md:463-471` | stays open, recorded | — |
| **F17** | **Req 4.8 (build hardening) and Req 4.9 (no secret-dependent control flow) have no executable pin.** Nothing asserts the hardening flags are applied; nothing tests constant-time behaviour. The only matches are comments. 11 of 13 security requirements are pinned by tests or tools. | v1–v3 | Med | conformance matrix below | 4.8 → `check_hardening.sh --require`; 4.9 → recorded as unpinned, argued by code review. **4.8 CLOSED** in V4-11 (`check_hardening.sh --require` on the install tree) and V4-15c (the `build_flags` CTest, F104). **4.9 partly pinned** in V4-15b: `tests/constant_time_sites.sh` fails if any of the 24 sites (25 since V4-20 added F48's owner comparison; the count is gated since V4-44) stops using a constant-time comparison, but nothing measures timing, so it stays open (V4-20). **V4-65: the first timing measurement**, of §7.3's decoy flow: `bench_decoy` times ClientHello→ServerHello for an enrolled, an unknown and a revoked handle, interleaved at random, with dudect's Welch t at five tail cuts. Revoked and unknown do not differ. Enrolled and unknown differ by about half a microsecond, shown only at 100,000 samples a class: **F135**, fixed in V4-67 by a 50 µs time floor on the lookup. One path is measured, not every site 4.9 covers, so F17 stays open | V4-11 / V4-65 (one path measured) |
| F18 | Plain `memcmp` on four attacker-influenced comparisons: the identity in a `.pub`/`.sk` file vs the expected id (×3) and the demo's echo comparison. All are local-file or plaintext-echo paths, not remote oracles. | v1/v2 | Low | `apps/demo_keys.c:225,449,614` (were `:310,396,513` until the references moved -- F101); `apps/demo_app.c:506` | accept with rationale; the daemon does not use these paths | — |
| F19 | **`-Werror=format-truncation` under gcc at `-O2 -D_FORTIFY_SOURCE=2`** in the `path()` test helpers: `snprintf(out, cap, "%s/%s", g_dir, name)` where `g_dir` is the same size as the destination, so gcc cannot prove the result fits. **Two sites**, found by sweeping every project source under that configuration (the one-site figure first recorded here was a spot-check, not a sweep). clang does not diagnose it and CI builds gcc at `-O0`, so **no existing gate sees it** — the same latent-Release+gcc class as F0. `test_authd_conn.c` uses the same pattern but writes into a 512-byte buffer from a 256-byte `g_dir`, which gcc *can* prove, and is unaffected. Fix verified in a container: checking `snprintf`'s return (truncation is a fixture bug → abort) compiles clean. | v4 (V4-6, V4-7) | Low | `tests/test_authd_keyfile.c:34`, `tests/test_authd_store.c:64` | **FIXED**: both `path()` helpers check `snprintf`'s return and abort on truncation (`tests/test_authd_keyfile.c:41-42`, `tests/test_authd_store.c:68-69`), and CI's Release+gcc job builds them on every push. *Corrected in V4-20:* this cell used to describe F0's `symlink()` fix (`176d786`) instead of this finding's | V4-11 |
| F20 | **Three spec gaps in §8 found while implementing it**, recorded rather than resolved by editing the spec: (a) no error code is defined for a refused/unknown command — the implementation returns `not-permitted`; (b) `ENROLL` carries no `role=`, so the role of a newly created user is implied by which command was used, and an existing user is never re-roled (`role-mismatch`); (c) list responses have no size bound in the spec, but are built in one buffer and sent as one reply, so they are capped at `AUTHD_LIST_MAX` (24) with `ERR code=too-many` rather than truncated. | v4 (V4-9a) | Info | `docs/mldsa-authd-spec.md` §8; `apps/authd/localapi.c` | **RESOLVED in V4-10c** — the specification is corrected; see its §20 Errata, which cites this finding. a spec errata step should adopt all three wordings; streaming lists if a deployment needs more than 24 | V4-9c / V4-11 |
| F21 | **`admin.sock` was mode 0660, but spec §8 says 0600.** `listener_open_unix` chmod'd every Unix socket 0660 unconditionally, so the administrative socket was group-reachable. The uid allowlist still refused such a peer, so nothing was exploitable — but the second layer is exactly what a second layer is for. | v4 (V4-8a) | Low | `apps/authd/listener.c:139-140` | **FIXED in V4-9b**: the mode is a parameter restricted to the two values §8 defines; mutation `S1`; asserted in `authd_e2e` because no functional behaviour changes | V4-9b |
| F22 | **A configuration failure exited 1, but spec §13 says 3.** `0 success, 1 operation failed, 2 usage error, 3 configuration error` is a contract an operator's start-up script depends on; nothing returned 3. | v4 (V4-8a) | Low | `apps/authd/authd_main.c:147,162` | **FIXED in V4-9b** in both binaries, including a bad passphrase file; mutation `C1`, which asserts *exactly* 3 — "non-zero" also matches a missing binary | V4-9b |
| F23 | **Two listener-failure paths leaked the decrypted server key.** They did `free(slots); return 1;` while `server_kp` held the ML-DSA secret in secure memory and `store`, `pending`, `conns` and `local_slots` were live — a Req 2 violation. The four sibling paths for the local sockets correctly `goto listener_failed`, whose epilogue wipes and frees everything, so this was an inconsistency rather than a missing epilogue. `evloop_add_listener`'s return was also discarded at two sites where the local equivalents check it. | v4 (V4-8a) | Med | `apps/authd/authd_main.c:292-296,302-307` | **FIXED in V4-9b**; mutation `R1`. The check is portable rather than LSan-based: macOS ASan has no leak detector, so the e2e observes the epilogue's *socket cleanup*, which is true exactly when it ran | V4-9b |
| F24 | **`store_backup` wrote the backup with the daemon's umask** — mode 0644 from an interactive shell. A backup is a byte-for-byte copy of the credential database: token hashes, the audit chain and every enrolled public key. Found by running the new `backup` command and looking at the file, not by reading the code. | v4 (V4-7) | Med | `apps/authd/store/store.c`, `VACUUM INTO` | **FIXED in V4-9b**: the destination is chmod'd 0600, like the store it copies; mutation `B1` | V4-9b |
| F25 | **§8 does not specify `ENROLL-OPERATOR`'s key set.** It says "everything above plus `ENROLL-OPERATOR`", and the implementation's `enroll_common` accepts only `via=site` — so operator enrollment, performed by the administrator, is audited as `detail="site"`. Confirmed by running `audit-tail` after a real enrollment. The spec is not edited to match the code. | v4 (V4-9b) | Info | `apps/authd/localapi.c` `enroll_common`; `docs/mldsa-authd-spec.md` §8 | **RESOLVED in V4-10c** as an erratum that adopts the IMPLEMENTATION: §8 now gives `ENROLL-OPERATOR` its full request line and states that it is audited as `via=site`, because the administrator running it is the enroller. The `via=operator` value `store_enroll_device` accepts is left unused — taking it would change an audit string the tests pin, for no gain in what the row records | V4-9c / V4-11 |
| F26 | **§3.1's handle format is a client-side convention, not a daemon invariant.** `authd_client keygen` generates `"d1" + 32 hex` and `test_authd_cli` pins that, but `id_valid` accepts any 1..64 printable bytes, and the existing Node fixture enrolls the four-byte handle `d1aa`. Nothing enforces the format at the boundary. | v4 (V4-9b) | Info | `apps/authd/localapi.c` `id_valid`; `tests/authd_node_fixture.c` | **RESOLVED in V4-10c** — the specification is corrected; see its §20 Errata, which cites this finding. Deliberate for now — handles are opaque to the daemon and the unguessability that matters comes from the generator. Worth stating in §3.1 rather than leaving a reader to assume a gate exists | V4-11 |
| F27 | **`bad-handle` and `ticket-invalid` appear in §8's error table but are never emitted.** A malformed handle currently returns `malformed`. Same family as F20. | v4 (V4-9a) | Info | `docs/mldsa-authd-spec.md` §8 vs `apps/authd/localapi.c` | **RESOLVED in V4-10c** — the specification is corrected; see its §20 Errata, which cites this finding. Fold into the same spec errata as F20 | V4-9c / V4-11 |
| F28 | **`AUTHD_PATH_MAX` (255) is roughly 2.5× `sun_path`** (104 bytes on macOS, 108 on Linux). A configuration with a long socket path passed every byte-level check and `--check-config` reported "valid"; the daemon then failed to bind at start-up. | v4 (V4-8a) | Low | `apps/authd/authd_config.h:27`; `apps/authd/listener.c` | **MITIGATED in V4-9b**: `authd_config_check_paths()` rejects it, and is called by **both** binaries so there is one validator with two entry points | V4-9b |
| F29 | **`store_audit_verify()` is reachable from nothing.** The audit chain's whole purpose is tamper detection, and the function that walks it exists and is tested — but there is no §8 command and no §13 subcommand that runs it, so a deployed operator cannot check their own chain. §9.2 says the head MAC "is written to the system journal periodically"; nothing does that either. | v4 (V4-7) | Med | `apps/authd/store/store.h` `store_audit_verify`; `docs/mldsa-authd-spec.md` §9.2 | **RESOLVED in V4-10c** — the specification is corrected; see its §20 Errata, which cites this finding. **IMPLEMENTED in V4-10c**: `authd_admin audit-verify` (`apps/authd/authd_cli.c:622`), offline, re-computes the whole chain and prints the head MAC and a verdict; §13 lists it and §9.2 tells the operator to run it on a timer. *The "Open" this cell carried until V4-20 was the V4-9b text, left behind when the closure was added* | V4-10c |

| F30 | **`store_rotate_key` would rotate the key of a revoked device or a disabled user.** It resolved the current key with `active_key_of`, whose SQL joins nothing (`SELECT key_id, pk FROM device_keys WHERE handle=?1 AND status='active'`), so spec §6.3's "re-read the store and confirm device and user are still active" could only have been honoured by a caller-side pre-check — which is a cross-process race, because `authd_admin` writes the same file. **Req 9 (revocation is immediate) would have failed.** | v4 (V4-7) | **High** | `apps/authd/store/store.c` `active_key_of` | **FIXED in V4-9c**: the three-join predicate runs inside `store_rotate_key`'s own transaction and answers `NOT_FOUND` for all three not-active outcomes; mutation `W7`, killed at the store layer where a unit test can see it | V4-9c |
| F31 | **A session established BEFORE a rotation could rotate afterwards.** The daemon verifies `sig_old` against the key the session authenticated with (correct — the digest binds `pk_old`), but nothing required the store's *current* active key to still be that one. A second session holding the superseded key could therefore commit a rotation that superseded a key its signatures said nothing about. Found while writing the mutation table, not by reading. | v4 (V4-9c) | Med | `apps/authd/authd_conn.c` `on_rotate`; `store.c` `store_rotate_key` | **FIXED in V4-9c**: `store_rotate_key` takes the expected old key and checks it inside the transaction; mutation `W4` | V4-9c |
| F32 | **`ROTATE` had no once-per-session rule.** §6.3 does not state one, and without it the holder of the key being rotated *away from* can keep rotating: `sig_old` still verifies, because the slot still pins that key. | v4 (V4-9c) | Med | `docs/mldsa-authd-spec.md` §6.3 | **RESOLVED in V4-10c** — the specification is corrected; see its §20 Errata, which cites this finding. **IMPLEMENTED in V4-9c** (`ERROR(0x02)` on a second attempt, mutation `W9`) and recorded as errata: the rule belongs in §6.3 | spec errata step |
| F33 | **§10.2's `.ek.next` recovery rule cannot be implemented as written.** It says "if it is unknown, the server never committed", but Req 6 and §7.3's decoy flow guarantee a client cannot distinguish unknown from revoked from bad-signature. V4-9c implements the sound contrapositive instead — **`.ek` authenticating proves the server did not commit**, and `.ek.next` failing proves nothing, so it is never deleted on ambiguity. | v4 (V4-9c) | Med | `docs/mldsa-authd-spec.md` §10.2 vs §7.3 | **RESOLVED in V4-10c** — the specification is corrected; see its §20 Errata, which cites this finding. Implemented as the inverted rule, with the reasoning in `authd_cli.c` and `decisions.md`; §10.2 needs the erratum | spec errata step |
| F34 | **§12 and §10.2 contradict each other on clobbering.** §12: key files are written "never in place, never clobbering". §10.2: rotation "renames it over the current file". The more specific rule wins for rotation, and `keyfile_promote` is the single documented exception — but the contradiction is in the spec. | v4 (V4-9c) | Info | `docs/mldsa-authd-spec.md` §12, §10.2 | **RESOLVED in V4-10c** — the specification is corrected; see its §20 Errata, which cites this finding. Errata | spec errata step |
| F35 | **§15 requires the `handshake_id` in the rotation log line; no logging function can carry one.** `authd_log_fp` is fixed at 32 bytes by its prototype and exists precisely so the never-list is a property of the API's shape. Widening `authd_log`'s byte-taking surface is the hazard F41 is open about | spec errata step |
| F36 | **The spec defines no rotation cadence.** §6.2 defines the `rotation_due` flag; neither §10.2 nor §17 says when it should be set. Record the knob in §17's limits table | spec errata step |
| F37 | **A ROTATE rejection is uniform in its error code but not in its timing.** §6.3 orders the cheap store query before the ~100 µs `mldsa_verify`, so "that key is already enrolled" remains distinguishable by elapsed time even though every rejection answers `ERROR(0x03)`. Reordering would contradict §6.3, so the deviation is recorded rather than taken. The peer is authenticated and the key is one it chose, which bounds what the oracle is worth. | v4 (V4-9c) | Low | `apps/authd/authd_conn.c` `on_rotate`; spec §6.3 | Accepted at V4-11. **CLOSED by V4-198** (spec erratum 102): the daemon verifies both signatures before the store is asked about `pk_new`, so the timing difference needs a valid `sig_new`, which needs the key. §6.3 and the code's comment now state that order, and `test_authd_conn` pins it | V4-11 |

| F38 | **The Node fixture keyed its temp directory on the pid with best-effort cleanup**, so every killed run leaked one, and once the OS reused that pid `mkdir` failed and `site_node_handler` died in about a second. Found when a mutation campaign failed its own CLEAN suite on an unmutated tree — the worst way for a gate to fail, because it reads as a real regression — with **1,214** leaked directories on the machine at the time. | v4 (V4-9a) | Med | `tests/authd_node_fixture.c` | **FIXED**: `mkdtemp`, so the name is unique by construction rather than by hoping the cleanup ran — the same reason both shell e2e tests use `mktemp -d` | V4-9c follow-on |
| F39 | **The recovery KDF blocks the whole daemon, and now there is a number for it.** Measured natively at §10.3's parameters (Argon2id ops 2 / 64 MiB, Apple M4 Pro, `-O2`): **52 ms** per verification, so `RECOVERY-USE` costs **0.82 s** at its worst (16 unused codes, no match) and `RECOVERY-ISSUE count=16` costs **0.86 s**. The daemon is one thread and one `poll()` loop, and `localapi_on_line` runs inline from `slot_readable` (`evloop.c:296`), so for that time nothing else is read, written, swept, or accepted, and every other slot's deadline is judged next iteration against a clock that jumped. A handshake already close to its 10 s limit can therefore be timed out by someone else's recovery attempt. **Accepted**, because the cost is bounded on three sides — at most 16 unused codes (issue supersedes the previous generation), at most 5 attempts per hour (the lockout), and the lockout is read before any hashing — and because 0.82 s is an order of magnitude inside `handshake_timeout_ms`. Revisit if the daemon ever serves more than operators. | v4 (V4-9d) | Med | `apps/authd/recovery.c`; `apps/authd/evloop.c:296`; spec §10.3 | Accepted and measured; three mutations (G4, G9, and the lockout pair) exist to keep each side of the bound honest | V4-10/V4-11 if load changes |
| F40 | **`ENROLL … via=site ticket=…` was accepted and the ticket silently ignored.** `ticket` was already in `enroll_common`'s allowed-key list (`localapi.c:251`) with no code path reading it — precisely the hazard `keys_are_known`'s own comment names ("silently dropping one would let a caller believe an option took effect"). A site that believed it was redeeming a ticket would have been performing an ordinary site enrollment instead. | v4 (V4-9a) | Med | `apps/authd/localapi.c` `enroll_common` | **FIXED**: `via=site` with a ticket and `via=recovery` without one are both `malformed`; mutation G13 | V4-9d |
| F41 | **`authd_log_fp` is the one never-list entry the logging API cannot enforce by shape.** Its prototype pins 32 bytes so it "can encode a SHA-256 fingerprint and nothing else" — but an enrollment ticket's hash is also `STORE_HASH_BYTES == 32`, so `authd_log_fp(…, thash)` compiles and leaks a live bearer credential to the journal. The guarantee is prose (`authd_log.h:62-69`), and `mutate_v49a.py:12-16` relies on that prose holding. | v4 (V4-9a) | Low | `apps/authd/authd_log.h:62-69` | **Recorded, not fixed.** A distinct `authd_fp_t` wrapper type would make it structural; that is a change to a header every daemon file includes, so it is proposed rather than taken here. Meanwhile mutation G12 makes the leak a campaign failure rather than a convention. **FIXED in V4-21** (user decision on the old mutations: re-author, do not allowlist): `authd_fp_t` is a struct that `authd_fp_of_pk` alone makes (SHA-256 over exactly the 1952-byte key, asserted against `MLDSA_PUBLIC_KEY_BYTES`), and `authd_log_fp` takes `const authd_fp_t *`, so a bare 32-byte secret no longer compiles; the journal line is byte-identical. Before: a 32-byte buffer passed to it compiled clean at `-Werror` under clang 18 and gcc 13. Pinned by the CTest `log_fp_type` (a canary that must compile, a bare buffer that must not) and `test_authd_log`'s independent SHA-256; campaign v63 FT1/FT2. v49c W12, v49d G12 and v60 GC4 now leak through an explicit cast, the one shape left, and the log scans still kill them | V4-21 |
| F42 | **§8 lists no error codes for `RECOVERY-ISSUE` at all.** Its row gives only `OK codes=…`, so the unknown-user and disabled-user outcomes are unspecified. The implementation borrows `invalid` and `user-disabled` from `RECOVERY-USE`'s vocabulary rather than inventing names. | v4 (V4-9d) | Info | spec §8 | **RESOLVED in V4-10c** — the specification is corrected; see its §20 Errata, which cites this finding. Add the error set to §8's table | spec errata step |
| F43 | **An operator's recovery had to be made administrative, and the spec does not say so.** `RECOVERY-ISSUE`/`RECOVERY-USE` are site-socket commands, but `ENROLL-OPERATOR` is deliberately absent from the site table (Req 11). Without a rule, a compromised site process could mint an operator's recovery codes, spend one, and redeem the ticket for a device it controls — the exact escalation Req 11 exists to prevent. Both commands now refuse an operator user on the site socket. | v4 (V4-9d) | **High as designed, closed in implementation** | `apps/authd/localapi.c`; spec §8, §10.3 | **RESOLVED in V4-10c** — the specification is corrected; see its §20 Errata, which cites this finding. **FIXED**; found only because the e2e's user happened to be an operator. §10.3 should state it | spec errata step |
| F44 | **Re-issuing recovery codes had no defined effect on the previous generation.** §10.3 does not say. Left additive, the `RECOVERY-USE` verify loop would grow by `count` on every issue, and F39's measured bound would mean nothing. Issue now supersedes, marking the old generation `used_from='superseded'` (never deleting it, per §10.3). | v4 (V4-9d) | Med | spec §10.3; `store_recovery_replace` | **RESOLVED in V4-10c** — the specification is corrected; see its §20 Errata, which cites this finding. **Decided and implemented**; mutation G9. §10.3 should state the rule | spec errata step |
| F45 | **`audit_append` stamps its own `now_unix()`.** Every V4-9d store primitive takes the caller's clock, as its siblings do, but the audit row it writes is timestamped from the wall clock — so a test driving an injected clock cannot predict its own audit timestamps. Harmless today (nothing asserts on them) and out of V4-9d's scope. | v4 (V4-7) | Low | `apps/authd/store/store.c:216` | Record; give `audit_append` a `now` argument when a step next touches it. **FIXED in V4-20**: `audit_append` takes `now`, and every primitive that has a caller's clock passes it -- rotation, enrollment, recovery issue, use and failure, and revoke=all, whose `revoke_device_locked` now stamps `revoked_at`, the key's `valid_to` and its audit row with the recovery's clock; the primitives with no clock read the wall clock once for every column they write. It was not only a test's problem: the daemon's clock is taken once per event-loop pass, so a row could already disagree with its own transaction by however long the pass ran (up to ~0.8 s through RECOVERY-USE). `test_authd_store`'s clock block printed six FAIL lines before the fix, with a wall-clock canary; campaign v62 AO1–AO3 | V4-20 |
| F46 | **§15 names two logging settings that exist nowhere.** `log_identities` and `log_client_ip` appear in §15's text; neither is a config key, a constant, or a field anywhere in the tree. Separately, §15's promised field vocabulary (`ev`, `conn`, `src`, `stage`, `status`, `ms`, `rx`, `tx`) does not match what `authd_log.c` emits (`event=`, `slot=`, `uid=`, `pid=`, `fp=`, `id=`, `detail=`). | v4 (V4-8a) | Info | spec §15; `apps/authd/authd_log.c` | **RESOLVED in V4-10c** — the specification is corrected; see its §20 Errata, which cites this finding. Reconcile §15 with what is emitted — the code is right, the prose is stale | spec errata step |
| F47 | **A refused `ENROLL` created the user anyway.** `enroll_common` created the user on first enrollment *before* checking the handle, so `ENROLL` naming a fresh user with an already-enrolled handle answered `exists-different-key` and left a brand-new user — and its `user-add` audit row — behind; likewise for an already-used public key. A refused request mutating the store is precisely what `fuzz_localapi`'s standing invariant asserts against, and it went uncaught for two steps only because **no fuzz seed carried a full-length public key**, so the fuzzer had never reached `ENROLL`'s store path at all (`pk=00` fails the length check first). Fixing the seed generator raised the target's coverage from 328 to 528 and it found this within 600 s. | v4 (V4-9a) | Med | `apps/authd/localapi.c` `enroll_common` | **FIXED**: every refusal that can be known in advance — Req 7 on the handle, and a public key already on record via the new read-only `store_pk_in_use` — is decided before anything is written; mutation G15 and a census check across the two refusals | V4-9d |
| F48 | **An idempotent re-enrollment does not check that the handle belongs to the named user.** `ENROLL user=X handle=H pk=P` where H already holds P answers `OK fp= idempotent=1` even when H belongs to a different user Y. Nothing is written, so there is no state to corrupt — but the reply asserts a relationship the store does not hold, and a site could conclude it had enrolled a device for X. | v4 (V4-9a) | Low | `apps/authd/localapi.c` `enroll_common` | Recorded, not fixed: the fix changes a reply the existing tests pin, and the request writes nothing. **FIXED in V4-20** (spec erratum 46; user decision: `pk-in-use`, no new code). The defect was in the store too, so it reached **via=recovery**: a ticket of Y's naming X's handle and key answered a plain `OK fp=` and stayed unspent. The store now answers CONFLICT unless the handle's device belongs to the user (`device_owned_by`, SQL equality), and the pre-check leaves an owner mismatch to it; nothing is written and no ticket is spent. Five named FAIL lines before the fix across `test_authd_store` and `test_authd_localapi`, with canaries (the owner's re-enrollment still idempotent, no user created, the ticket still usable); campaign v62 AO4 (the store) and AO5 (the pre-check), neither equivalent to the other | V4-20 |
| F49 | **The fuzz gate has been quietly narrower than it claims since V4-6.** `tests/fuzz/run_fuzz.sh`'s `maxlen()` was a hardcoded `case` covering only the original five targets, so for `envelope`, `authd_config`, `authd_conn`, `localapi` and `authmsg` it printed nothing, the command became `-max_len=`, and libFuzzer logged `INFO: -max_len is not provided; libFuzzer will not generate inputs larger than 4096 bytes` — confirmed in V4-9d's own `localapi-local.log`. Separately `maxlen wire` returned 8192 against `FUZZ_MAXLEN_wire=4096` in CMake, so the two sources disagreed in both directions at once. Nothing was unsound (each harness re-enforces `fuzz_target_max_len` itself, so no oracle was skipped), but every "600 s, 0 crashes" line from V4-6 onward describes a run narrower than stated, and the 4096–8192 range had never been fuzzed on five targets. This is the same defect class the file's own header comment congratulates itself on having fixed one function above, for the target list — the list was derived, the bound beside it was not. | v4 (V4-6) | Med | `tests/fuzz/run_fuzz.sh` `maxlen()`; `tests/fuzz/CMakeLists.txt:27-36` | **FIXED**: `maxlen()` derives from `FUZZ_MAXLEN_<t>`, and `check_bounds()` refuses to start when any target lacks one — an empty `-max_len` is exactly the silent degradation it replaces. Proven by a control (a bound deleted from CMake makes the run refuse) and by re-running all five affected targets for 600 s at their true bounds | V4-10 pre-step |
| F50 | **A WebSocket upgrade split across reads poisoned the connection.** `conn_io_next_frame()` calls `note_header()`, and the event loop calls it after *every* read — so while a WS connection was still buffering its HTTP request, the buffered `"GET "` was read as a big-endian frame length (1195725856), which exceeds `AUTHD_FRAME_MAX` and set the sticky `failed` flag. `push_ws()` already knew not to interpret those bytes; the second caller did not. The defect only appears when the request arrives in more than one read, which is what a real network does as soon as it crosses a segment boundary — and every hand-written test wrote the request in a single `write()` and passed. | v4 (V4-10a) | **High** | `apps/authd/conn_io.c` `note_header` | **FIXED** in the same step, before the code ever ran against a proxy: the guard lives at the single point that interprets `in` as a frame. Found by `fuzz_ws` on its first run; pinned by a named check that splits the request at every offset, and by mutation H7 | V4-10a |
| F51 | **An upgrade pipelined with its first frame was refused.** The `WS_UPGRADE_MAX` cap was applied to `in_len` — the whole buffered stream — rather than to the header block, so a peer that sent its request and its first frame in one write, or a proxy that coalesced them, was refused for a request that was ~160 bytes. | v4 (V4-10a) | Med | `apps/authd/conn_io.c` `push_ws` | **FIXED**: the cap applies to the header block, and to the still-incomplete case. Found by `fuzz_ws`'s chunking oracle; pinned by a named check and mutation H8 | V4-10a |
| F52 | **Bytes after a Close were dropped in one push and a protocol error in the next.** Both are safe, but they disagreed, so a coalescing proxy would turn a benign pipelined byte into a logged protocol error for data that can no longer affect anything. | v4 (V4-10a) | Low | `apps/authd/ws.c` `ws_consume` | **FIXED**: after a Close nothing further is interpreted, whichever read it arrived in. Found by `fuzz_ws` | V4-10a |
| F53 | **A vendored SHA-1 now exists in the tree**, for RFC 6455's opening handshake and nothing else. libsodium 1.0.22 ships no SHA-1, the project links no OpenSSL, and Caddy has no "WebSocket in, raw stream out" mode, so the daemon must complete the handshake itself. RFC 6455 §1.3 does not treat it as a security primitive: no secret enters it and its collision resistance is irrelevant to every property this daemon claims — its job is to prove the server read the client's header, defeating cache poisoning by intermediaries. | v4 (V4-10a) | Info | `apps/authd/sha1.{c,h}` | **Accepted and scoped**: `static`-scoped to the WS layer, pinned by FIPS 180-1 vectors and RFC 6455's worked example, with mutations H1 and H2. `git grep sha1` should only ever find `ws.c`. *Corrected (F102, V4-15c):* `sha1()` is extern, not `static` -- `ws.c` calls it and `test_authd_ws` pins it -- and `git grep sha1` also matches the passphrase word list, CMake and tests. The scope is now a check: `tests/no_hand_rolled_primitives.sh` fails if SHA-1's constants appear outside `sha1.c` or anything but `ws.c` calls it | V4-10a |
| F54 | **The test harness leaked the proxy-facing listener.** `h_start` opened the WebSocket Unix listener and `h_stop` closed the loopback, site and admin listeners and not that one, so every fixture in every daemon test leaked one descriptor and left one socket file in the temp directory. Nothing failed, because no suite starts enough daemons to exhaust a descriptor table — which is precisely why it went unnoticed for a step. | v4 (V4-10a) | Low | `tests/authd_harness.h` `h_stop` | **FIXED**: `listener_close(&d->ws_fd, d->ws_path)`, beside its three siblings | V4-10b |
| F55 | **`authd_client`'s raw transport recursed into itself forever.** `cs_send`'s non-WebSocket branch called `cs_send` where it meant `frame_send`, so `login --port` — the operator's SSH-tunnel path, the whole reason milestone A has a raw listener — overflowed its stack on the first frame it tried to send. It shipped in V4-10a and survived a full step because **every test and every e2e leg used `--unix`**, where `cs->ws` is 1 and the branch is dead. At `-O2` the recursion is a tail call, so a Release build hangs instead of crashing, which is the worse failure of the two. | v4 (V4-10a) | **High** | `apps/authd/authd_cli.c` `cs_send` | **FIXED**, and the gap that hid it closed: `authd_e2e.sh` now logs in over BOTH transports, and mutation J12 restores the defect so it cannot come back quietly | V4-10b |
| F56 | **`ERROR 0x04` is defined by the specification and cannot be emitted.** §6.5 defines it for a refused connection, but every `ERROR` is a SEALED RECORD (`fail_with_error` → `queue_sealed` → `session_seal`), and a connection refused for its address or its rate has no session and never will: `authd_conn.c:132` already says "no session exists yet: nothing to reply with". Mutation W11 additionally pins `0x04` as the WRONG code for the ROTATE-conflict path, so the byte is doubly spoken for. V4-10b therefore refuses with an HTTP status at the upgrade — the one channel that exists before a session — and `0x04` stays unemitted. | v4 (V4-3) | Info | spec §6.5; `apps/authd/authd_conn.c` | **RESOLVED in V4-10c** — the specification is corrected; see its §20 Errata, which cites this finding. Errata: either drop `0x04` or state that it is reserved for a refusal that a future transport can carry | spec errata step |
| F57 | **Req 11's wording is narrower than the use §7.2 makes of it.** Req 11 speaks of "Local API callers", yet §7.2 cites it for the PROXY-facing listener's peer-credential check — which is the load-bearing half of trusting a PROXY v2 preamble at all, since the preamble is only unforgeable because the peer that wrote it is the proxy. The implementation applies the check to all three sockets; the requirement should say so. | v4 (V4-3) | Info | spec §5 Req 11, §7.2 | **RESOLVED in V4-10c** — the specification is corrected; see its §20 Errata, which cites this finding. Given F56, the only implementable reading is "close the connection", optionally after an HTTP status while the connection is still HTTP — which is what V4-10b does: 429 for a rate refusal, nothing at all for a missing address. | v4 (V4-3) | Info | spec §7.2, Req 12 | **RESOLVED in V4-10c** — the specification is corrected; see its §20 Errata, which cites this finding. | F61 | **V4-10b implements only the first of §7.2's two mechanisms, deliberately.** `X-Real-IP` is not read and no code path can be made to read it. Two trust paths for one fact is one more parser to attack, and the header form additionally depends on the proxy remembering to overwrite a client-supplied value — a configuration mistake with no local symptom. Recorded as a deliberate SUBSET rather than a silent omission. | v4 (V4-10b) | Info | `apps/authd/proxy_v2.c`; spec §7.2 | **RESOLVED in V4-10c** — the specification is corrected; see its §20 Errata, which cites this finding. | F62 | **`AUTHD_SLOTS_POLL_MAX` bounds the two slot pools COMBINED.** `evloop_run_once` fills one `slot_ref[]` of 4096 entries from the protocol pool first and the local pool second, so a daemon configured at `max_slots = 4096` with every slot busy would never poll a local-API slot — the site would be unable to `EXCHANGE` precisely when logins are busiest, which is the failure the separate pools exist to prevent. Latent today: the default is 256 and the local pool is 8. | v4 (V4-9a) | Med | `apps/authd/evloop.c` `evloop_run_once`; `evloop.h:84` | **FIXED in V4-12.** `AUTHD_SLOTS_POLL_MAX` is now **derived** as `AUTHD_SLOTS_MAX + AUTHD_LOCAL_SLOTS_MAX` rather than restated as a literal, so a change to either maximum cannot leave it behind; each pool is additionally bounded by its own maximum inside `evloop_run_once`, and both pool sizes are rejected at registration, which makes the array bound unreachable by contract instead of by coincidence. Tested with a genuinely full 4,096-slot protocol pool — one quiet socket standing in for every slot, since `poll()` accepts the same fd repeatedly — requiring a local slot's request still to be delivered. Campaign v52's P10 restores the whole original block, because reverting only the constant does not compile | V4-12 |
| F63 | **A `--check-config` that passes does not mean the limiter is on.** `proxy_protocol` defaults to `none`, so a deployment that forgets the key gets a proxy-facing listener with no client address, no rate limiting and — since Req 12 only bites when an address is expected — no complaint. The default is deliberate (there is no value that is right for both "Caddy is in front" and "an operator's client is connected directly"), but it means the deployment checklist, not the config parser, is what stands between an operator and an unlimited login endpoint. | v4 (V4-10b) | Med | `apps/authd/authd_config.c`; `deploy/` runbook | Recorded and mitigated in documentation: `--check-config` now PRINTS `proxy_protocol=` and the four limiter values, so the setting is visible in the one command an operator is told to run | V4-11 **Further mitigated in V4-11**: `deploy/authd.conf.example` -- the one file an operator copies -- sets `proxy_protocol = v2` and says in a comment that this is the setting most worth checking, so the default is never what a deployment inherits by accident. |
| F64 | **`memcpy(dst, NULL, 0)` in the WebSocket fuzz harness.** libFuzzer hands a NULL pointer with a zero length for the empty input, and glibc declares `memcpy`/`memcmp`'s pointer arguments `nonnull`, so copying nothing from NULL is undefined behaviour. Linux clang UBSan says so; **macOS UBSan does not**, which is how it shipped in V4-10a and passed a fresh local UBSan suite. It only surfaced now because **V4-10a sat unpushed for a whole step** — the first CI run that ever saw that commit was V4-10b's push, and `ubuntu-latest·clang·ubsan` went red on `fuzz_replay_ws`. Test-only code; no daemon path is affected. | v4 (V4-10a) | Low | `tests/fuzz/fuzz_ws.c` | **FIXED**: the copy and both comparisons are guarded on `size > 0`. Reproduced in a Linux container by restoring the defect (`runtime error: null pointer passed as argument 2` at the exact line) and shown green after | V4-10b |
| F65 | **A commit that is not pushed gets no CI, and nobody notices for a step.** V4-10a was verified locally to this project's full standard — fresh ASan, fresh UBSan, campaigns, 600 s fuzz — and still carried two defects that its own CI would have caught on the first push: F55 (the `--port` recursion, which Release `-O2` turns into a hang) and F64 (Linux-only UBSan). The local gates are macOS-shaped; the CI matrix exists precisely because they are, and holding a commit back defers the half of the verification that is not reproducible on the developer's machine. | v4 (V4-10a) | Med | process | Recorded. The pattern to avoid is a *verified* commit waiting on an unrelated instruction; either push it or state plainly that its CI-only gates have not run | V4-11 |
| F66 | **§10.3 conflates two different guarantees in one sentence.** "Revocation takes effect at the next handshake immediately" is both "at the next handshake" (what Req 9 says, and what the store's three-join lookup delivers) and "immediately" (what closing live connections delivers). Errata: state both, separately | V4-10c |

V4-9d's decision 11 chose `none` as the default and implemented `all` as the stolen-device path; the spec never learned. | v4 (V4-9d) | Info | spec §8, §10.3 | **RESOLVED in V4-10c** — the specification is corrected; see its §20 Errata, which cites this finding. | F68 | **§17's limits table omits most of the document's own normative limits.** It carries six rows; the spec states at least ten more as requirements elsewhere — `AUTHD_MAX_CONTENT`, the 8192-byte local-socket line, token lifetimes and idle windows, the ticket TTL, the recovery lockout, `1..16` codes, the `VERIFY` cache TTL, `state` ≤ 64 — and, since V4-10b, four rate-limit defaults. A limits section that is not the place to look for a limit is worse than none. | v4 (V4-3) | Info | spec §17 | **RESOLVED in V4-10c** — the specification is corrected; see its §20 Errata, which cites this finding. | F70 | **§10.2 asserts "there is no overlap window in which two keys authenticate", and the interrupted-rename case it describes one paragraph earlier is arguably one.** The store's `one_active_key` index makes the assertion true of the SERVER at every instant; what the earlier paragraph describes is a CLIENT that does not know which of its two files the server accepted. | F71 | **The vocabulary gate silently skipped any log call whose LEVEL was computed.** `check_spec_vocabularies.py` anchored on a literal `AUTHD_LOG_[A-Z]+,` immediately after the opening paren, so `authd_log_num(ms == MEMLOCK_LOW ? AUTHD_LOG_WARN : AUTHD_LOG_INFO, "memlock-limit", …)` matched nothing and was not extracted. The gate then reported "53 names, all match" while looking straight past a brand-new, undocumented event — the exact failure it was built to prevent, one step after it was built. Its own "a non-literal at any of those sites is itself reported" claim did not hold, because a site it never found is a site it cannot report. | v4 (V4-10c) | Med | `tools/audit/check_spec_vocabularies.py` | **FIXED in V4-11**: the call is found first and its arguments split afterwards, so an unreadable call site is reported rather than skipped; and WHICH functions take an event is now derived from `authd_log.h`'s prototypes rather than enumerated, so a new logging function is covered the day it is declared. Found by V4-11's first new log event failing to fail the gate | V4-11 |
| F72 | **An offline dependency cache populated by one user and built by another is refused by git, and the reason is three layers down.** git's `safe.directory` rule rejects a repository owned by a different uid; with the cache used as a `GIT_REPOSITORY`, the refusal surfaces as `Failed to clone repository` from a generated FetchContent subbuild script, with the actual cause (`detected dubious ownership`) in a log the operator has no reason to open. Found by V4-11's own container proof on its first run — the cache arrived carrying the host's uid while the build ran as root, which is exactly the shape of an operator who populates a cache as themselves and builds as root. | v4 (V4-11) | Low | `cmake/Dependencies.cmake`, offline-build container proof | **FIXED in V4-11**: the cached clone is probed at configure time with one `git rev-parse HEAD`, which answers both whether git will read it and whether it holds the pinned commit; an ownership refusal is re-reported naming the fix (`chown`, not an exception), and a cache off the pin is refused before anything is populated. Both existing pin checks are unchanged, so an offline build is now verified three times and an online one twice. `deploy/fetch-deps.sh` and the runbook state the ownership requirement | V4-11 |
| F73 | **`cmake --install` deposited the vendored liboqs into the deployment prefix.** liboqs is brought in with FetchContent and defines its own `install()` rules, so installing the daemon also wrote `lib/liboqs.a`, `include/oqs/*`, a pkg-config file and CMake package files — a pin-locked, `-march`-tuned static library sitting in `/usr/local` where an unrelated build on that host could find and link it. Found by running the new install target and reading what landed, rather than by trusting that it installed what it names. | v4 (V4-11) | Low | `apps/authd/CMakeLists.txt`; `build/_deps/liboqs-src/src/CMakeLists.txt:184-209` | **FIXED in V4-11**: every rule carries `COMPONENT mldsa-authd` and the documented command passes `--component`, which leaves the dependency's rules in the untouched default component. Installing now writes exactly six files. `EXCLUDE_FROM_ALL` on `FetchContent_Declare` would be tidier and needs CMake 3.28, against this project's 3.20 floor — raising the floor to suppress a dependency's install rules is the tail wagging the dog | V4-11 |
| F74 | **`tests/caddy_proxy.sh` cannot run on Linux.** It streams the repo into its container with `tar -c --no-mac-metadata --no-xattrs`, and GNU tar has neither flag, so the script fails immediately anywhere but macOS. Latent rather than harmful today — it is a hand-run gate and has only ever been run from the developer's machine — but it is a gate that a second person, or CI, cannot execute. Found while making `tests/offline_build.sh` portable for the V4-11 CI bring-up, which had the same defect. | v4 (V4-10b) | Low | `tests/caddy_proxy.sh:70-73` | **FIXED in V4-14a**: `--no-mac-metadata --no-xattrs` are passed only when `tar --version` says bsdtar; GNU tar writes no xattrs unless asked. Executed under GNU tar 1.35 on Ubuntu 24.04: the new step archives the tree (370 entries) where the old one fails with `unrecognized option '--no-mac-metadata'`; the whole script still passes on macOS, 8/8. It is still not wired into CI | V4-14a |
| F75 | **`BIND_NOW` is present on Ubuntu's gcc whether this project asks for it or not**, so the V4-11 measurement "the one property actually missing was BIND_NOW" holds for clang and not for gcc. Found by the CI bring-up control rather than by the measurement: removing `-Wl,-z,now` turned the clang release job red (three properties absent) and left the gcc one green, because Ubuntu patches gcc's default specs to link `-z now`. Not a defect in what ships — the shipped binary has the property under both compilers — but it means the gcc job's green tick does not demonstrate that this project's flags do anything. | v4 (V4-11) | Info | `ci-bringup` run 35547268009; `CMakeLists.txt` hardening block | **Recorded, no change.** `check_hardening.sh` reads the BINARY rather than the build files, which is exactly why both facts can be true at once and why the gate is still the right one; the nuance is written into `docs/decisions.md` under V4-11 so nobody reads the flat claim later and concludes the flags are redundant | V4-11 |
| F76 | **The `pin-failed` log event became unemittable.** V4-12 replaced the daemon's per-connection scratch `keystore_t` with a bare pinned key resolved through a callback, so pinning is now a `memcpy` into a fixed-size field. `keystore_add` was the only thing that could fail at that point, and a `memcpy` cannot, so the event survived only in spec §15's catalogue — an event that exists in a document and nowhere in the code. | v4 (V4-12) | Low | `apps/authd/authd_conn.c` (the deleted branch); `docs/mldsa-authd-spec.md` §15 | **FIXED in V4-12**: removed from the catalogue as spec revision v1.3, erratum 29. Found by `check_spec_vocabularies.py` at the right moment — it reported `defined but NEVER EMITTED` as soon as the branch was deleted, so the change could not be committed with a catalogue describing an event the daemon can no longer produce. Keeping a tautological guard to preserve the event was considered and rejected: `decode_client_hello` already enforces the id bounds, so the check would have asserted `x <= x` | V4-12 |
| F77 | **V4-2 S2's ledger figure was wrong twice, and the spec cited it.** S2 measured one `find_slot` scan and multiplied by **four**; a successful responder handshake performs **five** full walks (sweep, `find_slot` and the placement loop inside `insert()`, then one `find_slot` each in `get_digest()` and `consume_success()`). It also timed the scan in a hot loop, where a real handshake's ML-DSA work evicts the 147 KB ledger between walks and every walk is cold. Measured end to end the ledger costs **137 µs** at capacity 2048, not the 79.6 µs §17 quoted — over the 100 µs budget S2 concluded it met. | v4 (V4-2, found in V4-12) | Med | `docs/decisions.md` S2; `docs/mldsa-authd-spec.md` §17; `bench/bench_authd.c` | **RESOLVED in V4-12**: the walk count was corrected while writing `HANDSHAKE_PENDING_EXT_MAX`'s comment, and the end-to-end cost measured rather than modelled — the bench compares handshakes by ledger **capacity**, since `find_slot` iterates `capacity` regardless of occupancy and an occupancy comparison measures cache effects instead (the first draft of the bench made exactly that mistake). The capacity limit stays 2048 by decision, with the cost stated: 24 % of a handshake, buying 205 handshakes/s against 1024's 102/s. Spec §20 erratum 30 | V4-12 |
| F78 | **Nine test binaries could lose their own evidence when a check crashed them.** Under `ctest` a test's stdout is a pipe and therefore fully buffered, and Linux ASan exits without flushing it — so every PASS/FAIL line still in the buffer when a later check aborted was never written. Campaign v52's P4 made that concrete: on macOS it scored `KILLED(1 named via abort)`, on the Linux nightly it scored `SURVIVED(BAD:)` with **zero** of its `Pext` lines in the log and stdout cut off mid-line exactly where ASan's report began. The rule already existed — `test_session.c` states it and sets `_IONBF` — but only four of thirteen test mains followed it. | v4 (found by the V4-12 nightly bring-up) | Med | `tests/test_{authd_cli,authd_conn,authd_evloop,authd_keyfile,authd_localapi,authd_store,authd_ws,handshake,vectors}.c` `main()`; nightly run 35883855914, job `mutations · v52` | **FIXED in the V4-12 bring-up**: every test main now sets `setvbuf(stdout, NULL, _IONBF, 0)` first, applied to all nine rather than only the one that failed, because the defect is in the harness shape and any abort-inducing mutation in any of them would lose its evidence the same way. Only the Linux nightly could have found it: the macOS tree the campaigns are developed on flushed, and a campaign's verdict is read from those lines | V4-12 |
| F79 | **Twelve of 180 mutations are killed by the compiler, not by a test.** The runner scores `KILLED(compile)` when `-Werror` refuses the mutant, which proves the compiler works and nothing about any check. In the V4-12 nightly (run 35901328130) twelve scored that way. **v24 M8** is legitimate: it trips a `_Static_assert`, which is what that mutation is for. **v24 M4** has rotted: its replacement names `KEX_KDF_LABEL`, which no longer exists, so it fails to compile for a reason unrelated to what it once tested. The other ten — **v26 Q5, v48a D6, v48b C4, v49a A1 T4 T5, v49b L2, v49c W1 W7 W14** — delete a USE rather than change a value, leaving a variable, parameter or function unused. W14 had scored this way every night since V4-9c with no test ever running against it. **And the count is platform-dependent**: v49b's C2 kills by name on the Linux nightly but is refused by Apple clang 21's `-Wuninitialized-const-pointer` (`cfg` passed uninitialised to a const pointer), so on the macOS tree the campaigns are developed on it is a thirteenth compile kill — confirmed on the pre-V4-13a tree, so not caused by this step. | v4 (found in V4-13a) | Med | nightly run 35901328130, jobs `mutations · v24/v26/v48a/v48b/v49a/v49b/v49c`; `tools/mutations/mutate_*.py` | **RESOLVED in V4-14a.** All ten re-authored so each changes a value and keeps every use (M4 on a live anchor: one character of the v2 label, same length), and each shown to compile under clang and gcc 16 before running. Result: **all ten killed by named tests**, v48b C4 and v49b L2 only after two new tests -- **they were the gap the compile kills hid**: no test drove a WebSocket login's non-empty `state` through EXCHANGE (`test_state_binding_ws`), and no test exercised `localcli_call`'s own list reader, since the framing check drained replies through the harness (`test_localcli_split_list`). Q5, T4 and W7 were killed by checks other than the ones their specs had predicted; the specs now name the real ones. v49b C2 re-authored so Apple clang no longer refuses it either. **And a gate instead of a habit:** `run_mutations_v2.sh` scores a compile kill `KILLED(compile)-NOT-ALLOWED(BAD)` unless `tools/mutations/compile_kills_allowed.txt` names it with a reason; the list holds one, v24 M8, whose `_Static_assert` IS the check | V4-13a (W14); V4-14a (rest, and the gate) |
| F80 | **The client's rotation recovery had no end-to-end test.** `client_resolve_key`'s `.ek` / `.ek.next` rules (spec §10.2) — use `.ek` and leave `.ek.next`; refuse and touch nothing; complete an interrupted rename — were covered only by `client_key_plan`'s truth table, never by a login that exercised a real `.ek.next` against a real daemon. A refactor of the client could have changed any of them unseen. | v4 (V4-9c, found in V4-13a planning) | Med | `tests/authd_e2e.sh`; `apps/authd/authd_cli.c` (now `client_cli.c`) | **FIXED in V4-13a**, before the refactor it guards: three e2e legs (USE_EK, REFUSE, PROMOTE). REFUSE asserts both files byte-identical before it checks the message, and kills W14 once W14 compiles | V4-13a |
| F81 | **The per-address rate limit is hostile to users behind a carrier NAT.** V4-10b keys the bucket on the client address (IPv4) or /64 (IPv6) with defaults of 5 a minute, a burst of 10 and 8 concurrent connections per address. Behind carrier-grade NAT thousands of mobile users can share one IPv4 address, and they would share one bucket. V4-10b's plan named this risk and said it would be "recorded as a finding for V4-13 rather than discovered there"; it was not recorded until now. | v4 (V4-10b) | Low | `apps/authd/ratelimit.c`; V4-10b decisions, "the numbers are config" | **MITIGATED in V4-13d** (spec v1.7 erratum 38; user decision: refund on success). A handshake that authenticates an ACTIVE key gets its per-address token back -- refilled to now, capped at the burst, once per connection, never the global bucket -- so logins no longer spend their neighbours' budget; failures and decoys still pay, and the refund needs the key's secret, so Req 6 is untouched. **The residual is now a §18 limitation**: one prober behind a shared address can still empty it for everyone there. Proven by `test_authd_ws` (five logins in a row at burst 2 all served; two decoy handshakes earn nothing and get the next connection a 429); campaign v56 MB11–MB15 | V4-13d |
| F82 | **Spec §14's key-lifetime sentence cannot be met.** "The secret key is opened into wasm memory only to sign `ClientAuth` and wiped immediately after" — but `handshake_initiator_init` takes the keypair (`handshake.c:525`), so the key must exist from ClientHello until ClientAuth is signed, one round trip; and rotation signs `ROTATE` with the old key after the session exists. | v4 (V4-3, found in V4-13a) | Info | `docs/mldsa-authd-spec.md` §14; `src/protocol/handshake.c:519-533` | **RESOLVED in V4-13a** as spec erratum 31: §14 now states the lifetime the protocol allows, which `client_core.c` implements and `test_client_core` asserts (owned key freed when ClientAuth is built and on every refusal, lent key's pointer dropped; `CC_KEEP_FOR_ROTATE` extends it to `cc_rotate_build` only) | V4-13a |
| F83 | **Two things the browser cannot avoid and the specification does not say.** (a) How the login code reaches the site: the CLI prints it for a human to paste, and §14 is silent on the browser's hand-off (a redirect, a `postMessage`, a form the page submits). (b) What replaces the `.ek.next` rule in IndexedDB: §10.2 is written in terms of files and an atomic rename, and IndexedDB has transactions but no rename-over; the rule "only `.ek` authenticating licenses discarding `.ek.next`" must be restated for two records in a store. | v4 (found in V4-13a planning) | Info | spec §10.2, §14 | **RESOLVED in V4-13c** (spec v1.6 errata 33, 34). (a) The page POSTs the code in a request body to its own origin; the site checks `Origin`, EXCHANGEs it with the per-login `state` it holds, and keeps the token server-side (`examples/site-node/server.mjs`). `browser_e2e` shows the code in none of the URLs the browser requested and the token never in a response; controls: the code also put in a URL, and the site returning the token, each fail those checks. (b) A pending envelope in its own committed transaction before `ROTATE`, one transaction to promote, and `client_key_plan` for the decision (`web/client/store.mjs`, `flows.mjs`); `web_flows` in Node and `browser_e2e` in IndexedDB drive the stale-pending and lost-ACK cases | V4-13c |
| F84 | **`authd_e2e` failed once, 1.24 s in, and the cause is unknown.** In nightly run 36007850109 (the V4-13a bring-up), v52's clean suite after P11's restore failed `authd_e2e` at 1.24 s — too early for any login leg. All eleven v52 mutations were killed, and the same tree passed v52 in the next two runs (36017740468, 36025224882), so it is intermittent. Leading hypothesis, **unconfirmed**: the script picks `PORT=$(( 40000 + ($$ % 20000) ))` (`tests/authd_e2e.sh:145`), which lies inside Linux's ephemeral range 32768–60999, so the daemon's bind can occasionally meet a port the kernel has already handed to an outgoing connection; that fails at start-up, which fits 1.24 s. The daemon already supports `listen_port = 0`. | v4 (V4-10b, observed in V4-13a) | Low | nightly run 36007850109, job `mutations · v52`, `P11.ctest`; `tests/authd_e2e.sh:145` | **FIXED in V4-14a, on the leading hypothesis.** The port is now `20000 + pid % 10000`, below both ephemeral ranges (Linux 32768–60999, macOS 49152–65535). The row's other idea, `listen_port = 0`, does not work here: in this daemon's config 0 means the listener is disabled. The failure itself was never captured, so this is a fix to the likeliest cause, not a diagnosis; F85 is what will keep the evidence if it recurs -- and in its first week it caught a different e2e flake, F89 | V4-14a |
| F85 | **A campaign's clean-suite failure keeps no evidence of why.** `run_mutations_v2.sh:121` runs the post-restore suite as plain `ctest` into `<ID>.ctest`, which records only the summary line for a failing test; the failing test's own output (its `FAIL:` lines, a script's `fail()` dump) is never written anywhere the uploaded artifact contains. F84 is the first time that mattered: a `CLEAN-SUITE=FAIL(BAD)` whose cause cannot be recovered from the run that produced it. | v4 (V3-era runner, found in V4-13a) | Low | `tools/run_mutations_v2.sh:107,121` | **FIXED in V4-14a.** Both whole-suite runs use `ctest --output-on-failure`. Control: a deliberate failing check reached `preflight.ctest` by its own line. **It paid for itself at once:** the first campaign run after it caught `authd_e2e` failing in a restored clean tree and kept the reason, which was F89 | V4-14a |
| F86 | **`tests/fuzz/fuzz_keys.c` does not build under gcc 16.** `-Wformat-truncation` rejects the `snprintf` at line 865 at `-O3`. CI's GCC 13.3.0 accepts it, so every current gate is green; it will surface as a red gcc job the day the CI image's gcc moves, in a file that step did not touch. Found while reproducing V4-13a's gcc-only failure locally with Homebrew gcc 16; identical on the pre-V4-13a tree. | v4 (latent, found in V4-13a) | Low | `tests/fuzz/fuzz_keys.c:865` | **FIXED in V4-14a.** Both `snprintf` calls check their length and fail closed: the secret scanner counts an unholdable path as a violation (control: a 1,346-byte path gives 1 violation, where it had been skipped; the repository scan is unchanged at 28 files, 0 violations), and the absence check counts it as found. A whole gcc 16 Release tree then builds with no error and no warning, and its suite passes 40/40 -- the first gcc-built macOS tree this project has run | V4-14a |
| F87 | **`check_build_current.sh` could call a stale tree current.** Its proof was a no-op rebuild — "a stale object cannot survive a rebuild unchanged" — which holds only if the build tool sees the change. macOS's `/usr/bin/make` is GNU make 3.81, which compares timestamps to the second, so a source restored within the same second as the previous link (a fast probe-and-restore loop does exactly this) left a stale artefact that make called up to date and the gate then agreed with. Found in V4-13b when a probe's extra export survived its own byte-exact restore and failed the next suite. Reproduced deterministically afterwards (probe built, source restored with an mtime older than the object): the old gate said `OK: current` with the probe string still in the object. | v4 (V2-10 gate, found in V4-13b) | Med | `tools/check_build_current.sh`; `/usr/bin/make` 3.81 | **FIXED in V4-13b**: the gate now deletes every project object, archive and executable (never `_deps`) and rebuilds, so the claim is "a from-scratch rebuild reproduces what is on disk" — the campaign runner's `forced_build` semantics, which was never fooled. Cost ~8 s per native tree. On first use it found the normal and UBSan trees stale (which artefacts was not captured); every suite was re-run on trees it had just proven current, and all passed. Any earlier result quoted from a tree touched within a second of its last link is only as good as that suite's re-run | V4-13b |
| F88 | **The reference site could not be deployed as its own documentation described.** `examples/site-node/server.mjs`'s command line (V4-13c) had no way to set `secure`, so behind the TLS proxy it expects -- where a browser sends `Origin: https://...` -- the Origin check compared against `http://` and refused every POST as cross-origin: sign-up, login, recovery, while the pages still loaded. Every test drove `startSite()` directly with loopback http, where the mismatch cannot occur. Its sign-up invite was also taken on argv, which spec 12 forbids for secrets because other processes can read it. | v4 (V4-13c, found in V4-13d) | Med | `examples/site-node/server.mjs` CLI entry | **FIXED in V4-13d**: `--secure` (Secure cookie, https Origin) and `--invite-file`. Executed: without `--secure` an https Origin gets `403 cross-origin POST refused`; with it, the invite from the file signs up (`200`, `Set-Cookie: ...; Secure`) and an http Origin is still refused. Found by writing the public runbook's step 14 and running it | V4-13d |
| F89 | **`authd_e2e`'s "no login code in the daemon log" check misread one code in 64.** It ran `grep -q "$CODE" authd.log`, and a base64url code begins with `-` one time in 64 -- which grep takes as OPTIONS. A code beginning `-V` printed grep's version and exited 0, a false FAIL (caught by F85 in the V4-14a v26 re-run: `grep (BSD grep, GNU compatible) 2.6.0-FreeBSD` on the line before `FAIL: the login code appears in the daemon log`); most other option strings exited 2 or read an empty stdin and exited 1, and the check passed without having searched anything. A secret-absence check that is vacuous one run in 64 is worse than no check, because it is believed. | v4 (V4-9b, found in V4-14a) | Low | `tests/authd_e2e.sh` (the final secret scan) | **FIXED in V4-14a**: `grep -qF -- "$CODE"`, and the recovery-code scans made the same (their Crockford alphabet cannot start with `-`, but a scan for a secret should never be parsed as options). Demonstrated: the old form with `-VzQ9abc` prints the banner and exits 0; with `-xyz123` and empty stdin it exits 1 unsearched; the new form exits 1 when the code is absent and 0 when it is planted | V4-14a |
| F90 | **Nine mutations are killed without any named check.** Their spec lines name a test but no FAIL text, so the runner scores `KILLED(0 named)` whenever the test exits non-zero, for any reason: v35 U2 and U3 (`bench_smoke`), v26 Q2 (`test_session`), v28 X1 X4 X5 X6 (`fuzz_no_committed_secrets`), v29 Y9 (`fuzz_replay_keys`), v24 M6 (`test_handshake`). Four of them die by abort. It is the same weakness as F79 in a milder form: the kill is real, but no one has recorded which property caught it, so a test that started failing for an unrelated reason would still "kill" them. Counted in the V4-14a nightly (run 36345474231): 223 of 233 killed by name, 9 this way, 1 compile (M8, allowlisted). | v4 (V2/V3-era specs, counted in V4-14a) | Low | `tools/mutations/spec_v24.txt`, `spec_v26.txt`, `spec_v28.txt`, `spec_v29.txt`, `spec_v35.txt` | **Fixed in V4-14d.** Each kill's log was read: all were real, and in seven the check that fired printed text the runner could not see -- the fuzz oracle and the key scanner printed no `FAIL: `, `bench_fail` printed `BENCH FAILED:`, and CMake re-wrapped the smoke script's `FATAL_ERROR` text across lines. All four now print a `FAIL: ` line, and each spec line names its check. M6 and Q2 fault before any check can print (a read past an exact-size copy; a length that wraps inside `sodium_is_zero`), so the runner gained one other form, `ASAN in <function>`: an AddressSanitizer report with a frame in that function. Counting empty spec lines rather than `0 named` rows found a tenth, v23 K4b, killed by a named check no spec recorded. The runner refuses an empty expectation before building, and `check_mutation_anchors.py` refuses one in CI (both shown firing on an emptied M6). v23, v24, v26, v28, v29 and v35 re-run in full on the ASan tree: 44 of 44 killed as specified, M8 by its allowlisted compile kill | V4-14d |
| F91 | **liboqs 0.16.0 is in range of an advisory published 2026-09-22.** GHSA-wh5q-mpc8-67wf: a heap out-of-bounds read in LMS/HSS signature verification, patched in 0.17.0. This build is not exposed -- `OQS_MINIMAL_BUILD="SIG_ml_dsa_65;KEM_ml_kem_768"` compiles neither LMS/HSS nor XMSS, and the built `liboqs.a` holds 0 symbols of either against 8 ML-DSA ones -- but the claim then rests on a build flag rather than on the version. Found by the first advisory check the project has done (docs/review/ADVISORIES.md). | v4 (V4-14b) | Info | `cmake/Dependencies.cmake` (`MLDSA_LIBOQS_COMMIT`, `OQS_MINIMAL_BUILD`) | **MITIGATED in V4-14d; the bump waits for a release.** liboqs 0.17.0 does not exist yet: as of 2026-09-28 the latest release is 0.16.0, and "patched: >= 0.17.0" names an unreleased version. Pinning an unreleased commit would break this project's rule that every dependency is a release, so the pin stays. What changed is that "not exposed" no longer rests on a CMake variable: `liboqs_minimal` (CTest) reads the built archive and fails if any LMS, HSS, LM-OTS or XMSS symbol is present, with ML-DSA and ML-KEM required as the canary. Controls: one LMS symbol added to a copy of the archive fails it; an empty archive fails the canary. Move to 0.17.0 when it is released. **Re-checked in V4-22a (2026-10-11):** liboqs's public tags are `0.16.0` and `0.16.0-rc1` only -- no 0.17.0 and no release candidate for it -- so the pin stays. **V4-38 (2026-10-20): a second advisory in range, GHSA-xv6c-h66p-6mfq / CVE-2026-104255** (2026-10-06, medium): LMS lock callbacks fail open (LM-OTS key reuse, forgery), and LMS verification ignores the parameter set, with three out-of-bounds defects on LMS signing; all of it needs `OQS_ENABLE_SIG_STFL_LMS`, which is OFF here. The built archive holds no LMS, HSS or LM-OTS symbol (its 21 `OQS_SIG_STFL_*` symbols are the generic dispatcher, with no stateful algorithm to reach), and `liboqs_minimal` already gates that. Also patched in 0.17.0, still unreleased (latest 0.16.0), so the pin stays | V4-14d (the gate); open (the bump) |

Demonstrated: a config with `site_socket`, `listen_unix` and `proxy_protocol = v2` and neither allowlist passes `--check-config` (exit 0). `site.sock` is then protected by its 0660 file mode alone, and the proxy-facing listener trusts a PROXY v2 preamble from any member of its group -- the self-reported address Req 11 exists to prevent. `authd.conf.example` names both, and the runbook says to, so a deployment that follows either is not exposed; one that does not gets no warning. | F105 | **The secret scanner counts a mostly public window as a secret one, and V2-8's "cannot produce a public-key false positive" holds for one key only.** `fuzz_keys --scan-secret` keeps the 45 windows that straddle a boundary of `rho`, `K` and `tr`, because each holds a secret byte. The 15 at offsets 17..31 are `rho[w..32) \|\| K[0..w-16)`; a file holding the public key holds `rho` followed by `t1`, so the window at 17 is in it whenever `K[0] == t1[0]`. For the scanner's single fixed fixture that is one fixed fact, and false -- which is what V2-8 measured. V4-15c's new scans copied the rule for FRESH keys and failed at that rate: two whole-suite runs of this step's own campaigns (v56 MB13, v49c W3) failed on `test_authd_cli`'s client-keygen scan, and a diagnostic run named the device's `.pub` and `sk[17..33)`.
